### The recorded choices

- **Item 2's home:** `products.py`, as the table says: the merge is a pure function of a state and a record, tested on the fakes without the CLI, and `baseline_record` stays the unmerged state for `configure --products … --apply`'s record (whose before and after must show what the application holds, not what the committed baseline held).
- **Item 3's two constants, whole.** `CREDENTIAL_KEY_PARTS = ("key", "secret", "token", "password", "credential", "cert", "private", "url", "push", "sqs", "sns", "webhook")`: a value under a key whose lower-cased name contains any of them is written as its JSON type, whether it is empty and the SHA-256 of its canonical JSON (sorted keys, no spaces), never the value; a matching key withholds its whole subtree as one digest. The prompt names `certificate`; the constant holds `cert`, which matches `certificate` and also Stream's `p12_cert` (the APN certificate field of its push configuration), a widening, never a narrowing. `VOLATILE_FIELDS = frozenset({"created_at", "updated_at", "duration"})`: the fields the comparison leaves aside, by name, at any depth. Three consequences, recorded: the rule reads the key's name alone, so `revoke_tokens_issued_before` (contains `token`) and a channel type's `url_enrichment` (contains `url`) are digested too, although they are no credential (both still read in clear in `baseline`'s snapshot); the server client's redactor has already replaced the values under the keys it knows before a body reaches the view, so for those the digest is the redaction marker's (said by `redacted_by_the_harness`) and a change of one non-empty value to another is not seen, only a change between empty and non-empty (no such marker occurred in V0: every redactable value was empty); and a boolean has two canonical JSONs, so its digest is reversible (`b5bea41b…` is `true`, `fcbcf165…` is `false`), which withholds a boolean's value in form only.
- **Item 4's commands.** From `services/api`, with uv 0.12.17 from the scratch environment, `UV_CACHE_DIR` under the scratch directory and the proxy and CA names by reference: `uv pip compile requirements-dev.in --python-version 3.12 --generate-hashes --output-file requirements-dev.lock --no-header --constraint ../../proofs/stream-chat/requirements.lock` (`Resolved 45 packages`, exit 0). uv takes the existing output file's pins as preferences, so every package the constraint does not name kept its version. The moved packages, before and after:

| Package | Before (B2's lock) | After (the harness's) |
|---|---|---|
| certifi | 2026.10.23 | 2026.7.22 |
| charset-normalizer | 3.5.2 | 3.5.1 |
| ijson | 3.6.0 | 3.5.1 |
| pydantic | 2.14.1 | 2.13.5 |
| pydantic-core | 2.50.1 | 2.46.5 |
| pydantic-settings | 2.16.1 | 2.15.0 |
| pyee | 15.0.0 | 14.0.0 |
| python-dateutil | 3.0.0 | 2.9.0.post0 |
| python-dotenv | 1.2.4 | 1.2.3 |
| **tzdata** | **2026.5** | **removed** |

  **`tzdata` did not stay as resolved** (a deviation from the prompt's and DM-17 5.1's expectation). B2's lock held it `# via python-dateutil`: `python-dateutil` 3.0.0 requires it, `python-dateutil` 2.9.0.post0 does not, and nothing else in the development set requires it on Linux (`pydantic` 2.13.5 names it only for Windows and its `timezone` extra), so uv dropped the orphan. Keeping it would need a hand edit of the lock or a new pin in `requirements-dev.in`, both forbidden; the premise that `tzdata` is a package only the API holds in its own right was mistaken. It is a development-scope package the runtime lock never had; `pip check` passes and every API check passes without it. The API's virtual environment was reinstalled from the aligned lock (`pip install --require-hashes -r requirements-dev.lock`: the nine packages uninstalled and replaced, `No broken requirements found.`), and the inventory was regenerated from it with a script reading `importlib.metadata` (name, version, scope by lock, `License-Expression`, `License`, the `License ::` classifiers, `License-File`), which reproduces the committed file's 45 unchanged entries exactly.

  **The README's documented command, run as written** (`uv pip compile requirements-dev.in --python-version 3.12 --generate-hashes --output-file requirements-dev.lock --no-header`, without the constraint) on a copy of the aligned lock: `Resolved 45 packages`, and the result is **identical apart from comment lines**: every package line and every hash is the same; the 93 differing lines are uv's `# via` annotations, which in the aligned lock also name `-c ../../proofs/stream-chat/requirements.lock`. So the command reproduces the lock's packages and hashes, and the README is not changed (the prompt forbids it); the constraint belongs in the command the next regeneration uses, or the comment lines drift.

### Reversals (each in a scratch copy outside the checkout; `git status` clean afterwards)

A runner modelled on `checks/fix_reversals.py` (not committed: `checks/` is outside this pass's owned paths) copied `proofs/stream-chat` to a temporary directory, reverted one change at a time with its pattern anchored once at a line start, checked that the reverted module still loads, ran the change's tests with the harness's interpreter (`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`), restored the change and ran them again. `reversals: 9, not demonstrated: 0`:

1. item 1, `verify` back to the five default types: `test_a_non_default_type_with_grants_is_a_difference` (`"agent grants for user not empty: […]" not found in […]`) and `test_a_type_stream_adds_later_is_a_difference_once_it_holds_a_grant` (`[] != ["later grants for user not empty: ['read-channel']"]`) fail; restored, OK;
2. item 1, `apply_plan` back to the five default types: `test_the_plan_locks_every_type_the_snapshot_shows` (`['commerce', 'gaming', 'livestream', 'messaging', 'team'] != ['agent', 'commerce', …]`) and `test_plan_enforces_checks_and_locks_everything` fail;
3. item 2, the merge's loop body replaced by `pass`: the four `MergedBaselineTest` tests and `test_record_products_baseline_keeps_the_committed_client_grants` fail (`[] != ['block-user-owner', 'create-call', …]`; the `_changed_paths` list begins with `feeds.feed_visibilities.followers.grants.…`; `'anonymous' unexpectedly found`);
4. item 2, the command writing `baseline_record` instead of the merged record: the `test_cli` test fails;
5. item 3, `withheld` writing every value: `test_credential_like_values_are_written_as_type_emptiness_and_digest` (`'AKIA-not-a-real-key' != {'type': 'string', …}`) and `test_no_credential_like_value_reaches_the_view` (`'AKIA-fake-sqs' unexpectedly found`) fail;
6. item 3, the guard passing every method: `test_the_guard_refuses_anything_but_a_get` (`None != 'full-view sends GETs only; refused POST users/{id}/deactivate'`);
7. item 3, the command not installing the guard: `test_full_view_writes_the_view_under_the_read_only_guard` (`None is not <function read_only_refusal …>`);
8. item 3, the comparison not leaving the volatile fields aside: `test_a_change_in_a_named_volatile_field_is_not` (`[{'path': 'app.body.duration', …}] != []`);
9. item 3, the comparison blind to a type change: `test_a_change_of_type_and_a_digest_change_are_reported`.

Item 4's two reversals are in the table above.

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **The live run stopped at item 4.1, as the prompt says it must:** a fifth addition on Stream's side, the Video call type `audio_call`, absent from B2's `baseline` of 14:39:44 UTC and present at 18:28:14 UTC. Class: provider-side configuration drift against the committed products baseline and the lockdown target, of the same kind as D2 and D3: Stream's default client grants (`user` 33, `guest` 6) in a call type the committed baseline does not hold. Its disposition (empty the client roles as D2, and record it in the new baseline as D3) is the manager's and the Dev Manager's, not this session's; `configure --products video,feeds` (a dry run) would plan two `PUT`s now, `agent`'s and `audio_call`'s.
- **Nothing was changed at Stream, in Notion or on GitHub** beyond the branch's three pushes. No departure from the prompt's procedure was made; two outcomes differ from its expectations and are recorded as deviations: `tzdata` left the development lock (item 4, above), and item 2's offline code is committed without the new baseline file.
- **Item 2's offline code committed on its own** (`2f54538`): the prompt puts item 2's code in the same commit as the new baseline file so that no commit names a file that does not exist. The file was never recorded (the stop), so the merge function, the command's use of it, their tests and the README's description are committed with `PRODUCTS_BASELINE` unchanged; no commit names a file that does not exist, and the constant's switch, the fakes' and tests' update and the README's file name wait for the pass that records the baseline. The alternative, leaving tested code uncommitted in an ephemeral container, was judged worse; the manager may drop the commit.
- **No Foundation run on the branch** (row 6). Class: a routing gap between the workflow's push filter and the branch names the session harness assigns (`ccr-…`); the workflow is not owned by this pass and was not changed. The offline checks ran locally with the workflow's commands; the run of record is the manager branch's or PR31's at integration.
- **The chat type `agent`'s stamps.** The snapshot reads `created_at` and `updated_at` 2026-10-23T14:50:33Z for the chat type `agent`, eleven minutes after B2's `baseline`, while B2's record gives `created_at` 2026-10-20T09:15:38Z. The two cannot both describe one object: either Stream re-created the type after B2's read, or B2's stamp was misread; B2's `.work` files were not kept, so it cannot be resolved here. Its roles and grant counts equal B2's (`user` 25, `guest` 21, `anonymous` 0, `channel_member` 22, `channel_moderator` 38, `admin`, `moderator`, `global_admin` and `global_moderator` 43 each, `global_read_only` 4). Class: a discrepancy in the record of a provider-side addition.
- **The two new Video call types are platform defaults.** `agent` and `audio_call` read `created_at` and `updated_at` `0` in Stream's listing, while the four known types read the lockdown's apply time (2026-09-27T05:58:30Z): Stream materializes a default call type at its first write, so the two were never written for this application, and the data holds no time for their appearance. Both read `lifecycle` `{"mode": "single_use"}` where the four known types read `reusable`.
- **`cert` in place of `certificate`** in `CREDENTIAL_KEY_PARTS` (above): a widening of the prompt's list, recorded; and the rule's two over-matches (`revoke_tokens_issued_before`, `url_enrichment`).
- **A boolean's digest is reversible** (above): `async_url_enrich_enabled` reads `true` and `moderation_keyframe_video_enabled` `false` through their digests. The rule withholds a boolean in form only; no credential is a boolean.
- **The comparison did not run live.** V0 was taken once; no second read-only view was taken after the stop, so `compare-views` has no live result and `VOLATILE_FIELDS` is unverified against two live reads (the fakes show `duration`, `created_at` and `updated_at`). The next pass should take two read-only views a minute apart and compare them before its first apply, so that any volatile field Stream carries that the constant does not name is seen on reads, not on an apply.
- **`npm ci`'s advisory** (row 3): B2's finding, named as the prompt asks; no change.
- **`uv`'s deprecation warning** for `UV_NATIVE_TLS` (the environment's variable): trivial, recorded.
- **The `.work` files** (`snapshot-20261023T182816Z.json`, `full-view-20261023T182818Z.json`, `usage-ledger.json`) are in the session's ephemeral container, ignored by git; this section records what they hold.
- **Local tooling:** uv 0.12.17 from the scratch environment; the harness's `node_modules` installed with `npm ci` for the tests; a scratch reversal runner, a scratch inventory generator and two scratch analysis scripts, none committed.

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **The stop at item 5.3** (above). Class: an apply's effect beyond its plan, found by the full comparison DM-17 2.3 added for exactly this. Stream's `PUT` of the default chat type `agent` re-created it as an application-level type, with a new `created_at` and the field `blocklist_behavior: "flag"` the request did not send; the nine roles' `scopes` follow the planned grants. Whether a re-created `agent`, with `blocklist_behavior` `"flag"`, is accepted as class 2's lock, and how the rest of B2-C2 (items 6 to 8) runs, are a new decision, not this session's. The development application now holds: the chat target (D1 corrected, the chat type `agent` locked; `configuration.verify`'s chat lines 0 at the apply's re-read), and Video and Feeds as B2-C1 found them (the call types `agent` and `audio_call` with Stream's default `user` and `guest` grants, D2 and D5, open). `conformance` would refuse to start (its `start_refusals`: Video and Feeds 4).
- **For the D8 question** (DM-18 item 8): in this run the first application write to a default type gave it a new creation time: the chat type `agent`'s `created_at` became the apply's second, while the two new call types, which no application write has reached, still read `0` (one observation, not a documented rule). A lock is therefore a write that replaces Stream's default with the application's own type; whether a later Stream re-creation could replace it again is not shown here.
- **No departure from the prompt's procedure.** Section 6 items 1 to 3 are done; item 4 (a rerun fix) was not needed. Nothing was changed at Stream beyond the one chat apply; nothing in Notion; on GitHub only this branch's pushes.
- **The geofences in no fixed order** (item 2): Stream returned the same nine geofences in three orders in three reads (B2-C1's V0, V0a, V0b). Class: provider behaviour against the comparison's positional rule; keyed by name under DM-18 3.3, narrowly (one path; positional fallback).
- **B2-C1's wording on `call_types` and `channel_configs`** (item 3): they are Stream's summaries of the same scopes, not the same objects; B2-C1's statement holds by name. Class: a precision in the record; no change.
- **The full view withholds the feed visibility `private` whole:** its name contains `private`, one of `CREDENTIAL_KEY_PARTS`, so V0a, V0b and V1 hold its type, emptiness and digest only (unchanged across the three). A change there would read as "changed", without the field. Class: an over-match of B2-C1's rule, erring toward withholding (as `revoke_tokens_issued_before` and `url_enrichment`); `products.verify` compares `private` in clear from the snapshot.
- **`compare-views` and `record-products-baseline` need the three names in their environment:** both call nothing, but the harness's `Context` loads the credentials for every command. They ran in the live command form. Class: harness shape, recorded.
- **`npm ci`'s advisory** (check 3): B2's finding, named; no change.
- **The `.work` files** cited here (`snapshot-20261023T210016Z.json`, `full-view-20261023T210018Z.json`, `full-view-20261023T210137Z.json`, `full-view-20261023T211507Z.json`, `compare-views-20261023T210145Z.json`, `compare-views-20261023T210518Z.json`, `compare-views-20261023T211509Z.json`, `configure-20261023T211451Z.json`, `usage-ledger.json`) are in the session's ephemeral container, ignored by git; the digests of withheld values stay there.
- **Local tooling:** a scratch wrapper for the live command form, scratch scripts for the classification, the listings, the plan check and the reversals (scratch copies made with `tar`, `rsync` being absent), all outside the checkout and none committed.

The Video and Feeds lockdown after B2-C2's chat apply, then B2's live rows (the brief, "B2-C2's live run stopped after its chat apply, and B2-C3"; DM-19). **The session did not stop: every step of the prompt's section 5 ran, in order.** Item 1's `baseline` showed exactly the four expected class-3 lines and no chat line; the two read-only views 69 s apart showed no change; the listings showed only the two expected marks; no case of section 3 arose, so no new baseline was recorded and no chat apply ran (V1 is V0b). The products dry run planned exactly the two expected `PUT`s; `configure --products video,feeds --apply` ran once, both `PUT`s 201, its differences after `[]`; the comparison V1–V2 showed 10 fields, every one within section 4 (4 planned grants, 2 derived `scopes` losses, 4 identity fields of DM-19 2.4 on the two call types the write materialized). Both counts were 0 before and after the conformance run. **The conformance run completed: 42 PASS, 2 RECORDED, 2 DIFFERENCE.**

**First, the blocking rule (DM-16 5.2): row D10 differs from the fixture.** "Deactivation of a user Stream does not have" gave `ok` where the fixture gives `member_unavailable`. Under the rule this blocks B2's merge until it is dispositioned. The run's own record shows why: the user the row names (the run's `U9`) **was held by Stream at D10**, because row M5 had just sent a message as `U9` and Stream accepted it (M5's `ok`, the other DIFFERENCE): read with the cleanup's count, that accepted send is what created the user at Stream (an inference from the run's own reads and the adapter's code). So D10 deactivated an existing user, and V13's `member_unavailable` came from Stream's 404 code 16 on that now-deactivated user, not from the adapter's "absent" read: **neither D10 nor V13 exercised "a user Stream does not have" live.** C4, which names the same `U9` before M5, did, through the read `deactivate_user` and `revoke_user_tokens` share. The analysis and a proposal are under "The two differences" below; no code was changed and no rerun was made (the rerun rule covers a run that stopped; this one completed).

The two tables above are the run's: the second lists every Glow code the adapter gave with what decided it (DM-16 4.5); a row whose outcome was `ok` or `already` has no code to map.

**The two differences, and R7.**

- **D10 (blocking; DM-16 5.2): deactivation of a user Stream does not have, `ok` where the fixture gives `member_unavailable`.** The adapter's `deactivate_user` reads the user first (`query_users`, deactivated users included) and raises `member_unavailable` when Stream does not hold it; it sends the deactivation only when the read finds the user (`chat_provider_stream.py`, `deactivate_user`). D10's `ok`, with no status or number, therefore means **Stream held the user at D10**. The user is the run's `U9`, the one identifier the rows use for "a user Stream does not have" (C4, M5, D10 and V13), never provisioned by the run. The cleanup found **one run-prefixed user the run had not recorded** (`users_found_by_prefix_not_recorded` 1; the run records only the four it provisions, `U1` to `U4`), so `U9` existed at the end of the run. Of the requests that name `U9`, in order: C4 (`create_channel`) sent nothing, because its read found `U9` absent (`member_unavailable` by the read; PASS); **M5 (`send_message` with `U9` as the sender) was accepted by Stream**; D10's read then found it; and V13's read found it too, now deactivated, so the adapter sent the revocation and Stream answered 404 with code 16, which the adapter maps to `member_unavailable` (the deactivated-user answer D9 also shows). **Read together, M5's accepted send created `U9` at Stream** (an inference from these reads and the code; Stream's documentation was not consulted). So D10 did not exercise its cause live, and V13's equal code came through another cause (a deactivated user); C4 did exercise "a user Stream does not have", through the same `_users` read that `deactivate_user` and `revoke_user_tokens` begin with. **Under the blocking rule the difference is reported here and blocks B2's merge until it is dispositioned.** A proposal for that decision (not made here; it is code outside this pass's paths unless a rerun is authorized): give each cause row its own never-used identifier (or make M5 after D10 and V13), offline in `conformance.py` with a test, then one more live run of the rows; the alternative is to accept C4's read as the live evidence for the adapter's absent-user path and record D10 and V13 as not exercising their cause.
- **M5: a send by a user Stream does not have, `ok` where the fixture gives `member_unavailable`** (a finding; reported, not fixed). Stream's server-side send accepted a sender it did not hold, into a channel of which that user was not a member, and (above) created the user. For P11's delivery: a send for a sender whose identity is not provisioned is not refused by Stream and creates a bare user outside the provisioning event; only Glow's own checks (D5) and the order of delivery (provisioning before the channel, B1's 3.2) keep that from happening.
- **R7 (recorded either way): a send by a removed member, after the removal: Stream accepted it (`ok`)**; the fixture refuses it (`member_unavailable`). Stream's server-side send does not check membership. As DM-16 4.7 says, this is a reported difference, not a failure: Glow's own send checks refuse it first (D5), and P11's worker must know that Stream will not.
- **V7 (recorded): a token whose `iat` equals the sent cut-off was accepted** (connect and read succeeded), while V6's token, whose `iat` is the cut-off's exact second, one second earlier, was refused (401, code 40). So Stream revokes a token whose `iat` is strictly before the cut-off it holds, and the delivery's rounding of the cut-off up to the next whole second, strictly (F1 point 3; DM-15 5.2), is what refuses a token issued in the bump's own second. V4: the cut-off Stream holds is the exact second plus one, as sent.
- **The signer** (T1): `iat` is the grant's issue time truncated to the second (`iat` − truncated issue 0; `IAT_BACKDATE_SECONDS` 0), `exp` the grant's expiry truncated (`exp` − `iat` 3600), claims exactly `exp`, `iat` and `user_id`, HS256; T2 shows Stream accepts it (connect, and a channel read answered 201). No backdate was needed.

**The provider halves of F3 and CX6** (the blocking rule's rows): C4 `member_unavailable` (PASS, by the read), R8 `channel_unavailable` (PASS, by the read), **D10 `ok` (DIFFERENCE, blocking; its cause not exercised)**, V13 `member_unavailable` (PASS in code, 404 code 16, through a deactivated user; its cause not exercised). Offline, a transport failure and a timeout give `provider_unavailable` (B2's tests, unchanged).

No current path reads it: delivery keeps only the code, the harness keeps code, status and number, and traceback formatting honours the suppression. The module docstring says the chain is dropped, and the test at `tests/test_chat_provider_stream.py:748` asserts only `__cause__`. | **B2-C4.** Raise the mapped error after the `except` block, so `__context__` is `None`, and assert `__context__ is None` in the boundary tests. |

It propagates, and delivery maps it to `provider_unavailable` and retries, then dead-letters after five attempts. Stream's real envelopes carry the fields (M4, D9, V13 mapped). | **B2-C4:** a sentence in the module docstring and a test modelled on the 2xx one. **Recorded here** as the mapping note: the boundary maps only the SDK's own errors (DM-16 4.1), so such a `KeyError` is retryable, as the 2xx case is (DM-17 item 5). |

That second option changes design point d ("removal of both members", DM-16 4.2), so it goes to the Dev Manager first. Not in B2-C4. |

### Deviations, findings and limits

- **No deviation, and no departure stopped at.** `checks/run_plan.py` is unchanged because its count is measured, not written (item 2).
- **A finding outside this work (an operability finding; not a fix-here class: no credential, request outside the guard or provider text is involved).** Cleanup's poll verification (`proof_run.py`, `_verify_polls`) lists polls as each run user that is not deactivated, and M5's sender is now a run user. If a live send did **not** create the sender, that listing asks Stream's Query Polls as a user Stream does not hold. Stream's answer to that is not shown. If Stream refused it, the polls would read "not verified" and `cleanup_problems` would report `remaining_polls: not verified …`: a false problem and exit 4, with every object still deleted. A scratch probe on the fakes, with Query Polls refusing an absent user, showed exactly that line. In the case B2-C3's live run inferred (the send creates the user, AM7-06), the sender exists at cleanup and the listing is as for the other users. Not fixed: cleanup's rules and `proof_run.py` are outside B2-C4. For the manager: either accept it (an exit 4 on that path is diagnosable from the record), or have a later pass skip, in `_verify_polls`, a recorded user that a complete listing does not show, as `_delete_users` already does.
- **Limits.**
  - The new tests run on hand-written fakes and the recorded transport; no row was run live. Whether Stream creates M5's sender stays an inference (AM7-06).
  - `__context__` is `None` when the adapter's caller is not itself handling an exception; otherwise Python sets it to the caller's exception, never to the SDK's (the docstring says so).
  - B2R2 covers `results()`, which feeds the JSON and Markdown records. `cmd_conformance` (`cli.py`, not owned) adds `stop_reason` after `results()` and prints `cleanup:`, `stop recorded:` and `after the run:` lines to the console; those are not generalized by this change. `stop_reason` repeats an exception's redacted text, which `run.stops` (generalized) also holds.
  - uv was installed by version from the package index, not by hash, as in B2-C1.
  - The reversals ran in full scratch copies of the working tree outside the checkout; `git status` was clean afterwards.

### What the exact-head re-review must know

- The adapter's change is confined to where the mapped error is raised (`:140-148`, `:247-257`); `_mapped`, `_send_error`, `_reports_existing`, the caught tuple and every code are unchanged, and `send_message`'s success path moved into the `else` clause unchanged.
- M5's sender is now reserved and recorded (`_new_user`), which adds one user to the conformance count (5) and the reserve (10), and one Query Polls call in cleanup (95 calls on the fakes); see the finding above for the case where the send does not create the user.
- The four keys are `U5` (C4), `U6` (M5), `U7` (D10), `U8` (V13).
- `results()` now generalizes every identifier of the run anywhere in the result; `render()` reads the generalized result.
- The run of record is 40049721647 on `42e6231`; the records commit is Markdown-only.

**Findings**

| ID | Severity | Finding | Disposition (App Manager 7) |
|---|---|---|---|
| C4R1 | should fix; the record-claim class | `_without_ids` (`conformance.py:867-883`) matches each identifier only in full. Strings that `proof_run._generic` has already rewritten hold `{prefix}<32 hex>`, so they keep their random part: `_text` (`proof_run.py:490-491`, used for `connect_note`, `read_note` and `error` in `_token_check`, `conformance.py:477-497`), `_client_note` (`:544-545`), the async-error and request lines (`:551-565`) and `_defer_stop`. Four statements claim more: `_without_ids`'s docstring, the harness README, the B2-C4 record's item 4 and its "What the exact-head re-review must know", and B2R2's disposition. | **B2-C5, in code:** `_without_ids` also replaces the form `_generic` leaves (`{prefix}` plus the identifier's part after the prefix), with a test that sends identifiers through `_text`, `_client_note` and a connect note; the claims state what the code then does. |
| C4R2 | nit, test strength | The `at_write=True` case of `test_streams_error_answer_is_mapped_with_no_cause_or_context` (`tests/test_chat_provider_stream.py:803-833`) relies on hand-counted reads and never asserts that the scripted refusal reached the write. The counts are right today. | **B2-C5:** assert the last recorded request's method and path, or the recording's length. |

**Manager verification (App Manager 7, 24 October 2026).**
- **The report:** it states the prompt's revision, its commit and the head as the prompt names them.
- **C4R1, confirmed in the code at `5b68e3c`,** whose code paths equal `da3c0ff`'s:
  - `_check` (`proof_run.py:486-489`), `_text` (`:491-492`), `_client_note` (`:544-545`) and the async-error notes (`:551`) each pass their text through `_generic`, which replaces the prefix with `{prefix}`.
  - `_without_ids` (`conformance.py:867`) builds its pairs from `self.ids`' full values only.
- **`stop_reason`, B2C4-X2's part that reaches a record.** `cmd_conformance` builds `stop_reason` from an exception's text through the redactor only (`cli.py:698-705`). It adds it to the early results (`:708-717`) and to the final results (`:740-742`), after `run.results()`. Both are written to `conformance-<prefix>.json`, and the final one also through `render` to the `.md`. So a stop whose exception names a run identifier writes that identifier whole. That makes "every part of the results that is written to a record" untrue for `stop_reason` too.
  - **Disposition:** it rides with C4R1 in B2-C5, as the reviewer suggests: `stop_reason` goes through `run._without_ids` before both writes.
  - The console lines (`cleanup:`, `stop recorded:`, `after the run:`) are not records. They stay carried to P11 with B2C4-X1.
- **The manager's own overclaim (AM7-08).** PR31's body and Implementation Control repeated the session's "every run identifier generalized" without checking strings already passed through `_generic`. Both are corrected with this record.
- **Effect on recorded results:** none. B2-C3's live record is unchanged. The exposure is the random part of synthetic, deleted users' identifiers, in the git-ignored `.work`.
